OUTBOUND LOAD BUILDING
Overview
Overview
AccellosOne 3PL’s load building program allows you to assign loads to specific doors in your warehouse and 
to sequence the load in a logical order using stops. If product is found to be damaged and cannot be shipped, 
it can be unloaded from a load and either assigned to another load or returned to the warehouse. A load in 
AccellosOne 3PL can contain product belonging to any customer. 
There are a minimum of six steps to follow in outbound load building:
The following restrictions apply to outbound load building:
 in pallet pick mode, you cannot have multiple pallet ID’s on the same load
 in case pick mode, you cannot assign the same outbound pallet ID to two or more orders
ENOR
SELO
SELO
SELO
You enter your orders in ENOR.
You create your load in SELO and then
attach the appropriate orders to the
appropriate stops.
You change the status of the load in
SELO to “ready to load”.
If required, you change the status of
the load in SELO to “complete”.
You confirm the load in CHOF.
You load the load in OLOP.
OLOP
RF
CHOF
You pick your order lines in RF.

OUTBOUND LOAD BUILDING
Setting Up Outbound Load Building
OPERATIONS 2 GUIDE 4.2* 321
Setting Up Outbound Load Building
There are five setup programs for outbound load building:
 BLDG (Building)
 DOOR (Door)
 FLPR (Flow Process)
 WAPC (Warehouse Attribute Profile Code)
 DOCU (Documents)
SETTING UP YOUR BUILDINGS IN BLDG
See “Setting Up Your Buildings in BLDG” on page 6. 
SETTING UP YOUR DOORS IN DOOR
See “Setting Up Your Doors in DOOR” on page 11.
SETTING UP YOUR WAREHOUSE ACTIVITY TYPE IN FLPR
Outbound load building requires the warehouse activity code of 22 (Loading) to be attached to the following 
two flows: 
 the last picking flow process code (for example, FIPI or ENPI)
 the first loading flow process code (for example, STLO for Start Loading)
1 Enter FLPR.
2 Make sure that you have at least one flow process code for loading.
3 Position your cursor over your loading flow process code. If you have more than one flow process code 
for loading, position your cursor over the first flow process code for loading (for example, STLO for Start 
Loading).

OUTBOUND LOAD BUILDING
Setting Up Outbound Load Building

Flow Process (FLPR) screen showing flow for start loading
4 Click on Activity Block.
5 Click on Create Record.
6 Key in 22 (Loading) as your warehouse activity and press Enter.
7 Press Enter again to confirm the new activity.

Flow Process (FLPR) screen showing Activity Block
8 Click on Return to Main to exit create record mode. Then click on Master Block.
9 Repeat the above steps for your last flow process code for picking.
10 When you finish setting up the Activity Block for both flows, click on Exit to exit.

OUTBOUND LOAD BUILDING
Setting Up Outbound Load Building
OPERATIONS 2 GUIDE 4.2* 323
SETTING UP YOUR WAREHOUSE ATTRIBUTE CODES IN WAPC
Warehouse attributes describe the various loading options in AccellosOne 3PL; for example, verify the door 
for every scan, blind count at loading required/not required, automatically set the load’s status to complete, 
unique identifier/search by level 3, etc.
You can have any combination of warehouse attributes, each of which is identified under a unique warehouse 
attribute code that is attached to your load in SELO. You can use the same code for each load or you can vary 
the code depending on the characteristics of the load, the stops on the load, the orders on the load or any 
other criteria that makes sense for your warehouse.
The warehouse attribute profile code of DEVF is a system code and cannot be deleted. It is your default code 
and will automatically populate the Warehouse Attribute field in SELO. You can change your default code by 
means of the Update command.
Outbound loading supports the following attributes. 
ATTRIBUTE 
TYPE OPTIONS
300 0 = Verify door only once*
1 = Verify door on every scan
If you select 0, you enter the door once for each load in OLOP. If you select 1, 
you must enter the door for each scan.
* Not available for loading by load
301 0 = Blind count not required
1 = Blind count required
If you select 0, you are not prompted to enter the quantity in OLOP. If you select 
1, you are prompted to enter the quantity in OLOP and you cannot proceed if the 
quantity is incorrect. You are allowed two attempts to enter the correct quantity. If 
the second attempt fails, the product cannot be loaded until a supervisor logs on 
and accepts the system quantity.
If the supervisor accepts the system quantity, the pallet or order line can be 
loaded and AccellosOne 3PL will create a record in the Time Block of LOOR 
advising that there was a supervisor override. NO ADJUSTMENT TO INVENTORY QUANTITES WILL OCCUR. If the supervisor rejects the system quantity, 
the pallet or order line cannot be loaded at this time.
302 0 = Load by order sequence (reserved for future use)
1 = Load by stop number
Set to 1.

OUTBOUND LOAD BUILDING
Setting Up Outbound Load Building
303 0 = Pop-up for deletion not required
1 = Pop-up for deletion required
If you select 0, you will not be prompted to confirm the removal of an order in 
SELO or the unloading of a pallet in OLOP. If you select 1, you will be prompted 
to confirm these actions.
304 0 = UI. Search by Level 1 not required
1 = UI. Search by Level 1 required
This warehouse parameter together with parameters 305, 306 and 307 allow 
you to define which inventory levels are unique so that queries in OLOP are performed in the most efficient way possible. For example, if all your customers in a 
given company were three-level accounts with a unique pallet ID as level 3, you 
would want to perform queries in OLOP by searching for unique level 3 values. 
Searching for level 1 and 2 values would be inefficient and time consuming.
At least one parameter (304, 305, 306 or 307) should be set to the default (1). If 
none of your inventory levels are unique, all four parameters should be set to the 
default.
EXAMPLE 1
Level 2 is unique for all accounts
304 = 0
305 = 1
306 = 0
307 = 0
EXAMPLE 2
Level 3 is unique for all accounts 
304 = 0
305 = 0
306 = 1
307 = 0
ATTRIBUTE 
TYPE OPTIONS

OUTBOUND LOAD BUILDING
Setting Up Outbound Load Building
OPERATIONS 2 GUIDE 4.2* 325
EXAMPLE 3
Level 2 is unique for some accounts while level 3 is unique for the remaining 
accounts*
304 = 0
305 = 1
306 = 1
307 = 0
* If the unique inventory level varies by account, you could set up two or more warehouse 
attribute codes; for example, code 1 with the attribute 305 set to 1 and code 2 with the attribute 306 set to 1.
305 0 = UI. Search by Level 2 not required
1 = UI. Search by Level 2 required
See warehouse parameter 304.
306 0 = UI. Search by Level 3 not required
1 = UI. Search by Level 3 required
See warehouse parameter 304.
307 0 = UI. Search by Level 4 not required
1 = UI. Search by Level 4 required
See warehouse parameter 304.
310 0 = Do not prompt for entry of carrier details in OLOP
1 = Prompt for entry of carrier details in OLOP
If you select 0, carrier details will not display. If you select 1, carrier details will 
display and the RF operator will be prompted to enter the carrier code, power 
unit number, seal numbers and temperatures.
311 0 = Pallet information entry not required
1 = Pallet information entry required
Reserved for future use.
312 0 = Order line picklist not required
1 = Order line picklist required
Reserved for future use.
ATTRIBUTE 
TYPE OPTIONS

OUTBOUND LOAD BUILDING
Setting Up Outbound Load Building
313 0 = Allow advance of order flow by order number
1 = Allow advance of order flow by load number only
If you select 0, you can advance the flow of an order in CHOF by order number 
even if it is on a load. If you select 1, you can advance the flow of an order in 
CHOF by load number.
314 0 = Advance the load status to confirm manually
1 = Advance the load status to confirm automatically
If you select 0, the load will NOT be automatically confirmed when you confirm 
the last order on the load in CHOF. If you select 1, the load will be automatically 
confirmed when you confirm the last order on the load in CHOF. 
315 0 = Advance the complete status of a load manually
1 = Advance the complete status of a load automatically
If you select 0, you must change the status of a load in SELO from “Loading” to 
“Complete” after performing loading. If you select 1, the load’s status will be 
automatically advanced to “Complete” after you finish loading.
NOTE If you select 1, you cannot unload a completed load in OLOP.
316 0 = Launch loading by DOOR (reserved for future use)
1 = Launch loading by LOAD
This attribute is global. It applies to all attribute codes in your company. For 
example, if you set this attribute to 1 in company W1, it will apply to all warehouse attribute codes in W1. You cannot set this attribute to 0 for warehouse 
attribute code 1 and to 1 for warehouse attribute code 2.
317 0 = Default entry by load number
1 = Default entry by load number external reference
If you select 0, the cursor will be positioned in the Load Number field when you 
enter OLOP. If you select 1, the cursor will be positioned in the External Reference Number field when you enter OLOP.
318 0 = Stop number not required
1 = Stop number required
If you select 0, the stop number is not required when the status of a load is set to 
Create. If you select 1, the stop number is required when the status of a load is 
set to Create. When load’s status changes to Ready to Load, the stop number is 
mandatory regardless of the value of warehouse attribute 318.
ATTRIBUTE 
TYPE OPTIONS

OUTBOUND LOAD BUILDING
Setting Up Outbound Load Building
OPERATIONS 2 GUIDE 4.2* 327
319 0 = Print either outbound pallet ID or inventory levels
1 = Print both outbound pallet ID and inventory levels
If you select 0, the load sheet that you print in SELO will show either the outbound pallet ID (if any) or all inventory levels. If you select 1, the load sheet that 
you print in SELO will print both the outbound pallet ID (if any) and all inventory 
levels.
320 0 = No pallet position capture required
1 = Capture pallet position manually
2 = Capture pallet position automatically
If you select 0, no pallet positions will be generated and operators cannot manually enter a pallet position in OLOP. If you select 1 (Capture pallet position manually), operators must manually enter the pallet positions on a load. If you select 
2 (Capture pallet position automatically), AccellosOne 3PL will automatically 
generate pallet positions for each pallet on a load and operators can manually 
override these system-generated positions if necessary.
Pallet positions are defined in attribute 336.
321 0 = Print Load Plan Sheet manually
1 = Print Load Plan Sheet automatically
If you select 0, the load plan sheet must be printed manually in SELO or OLOP. 
If you select 1 (Print load sheet automatically), the load plan sheet will be printed 
automatically when the load's status is marked as complete. 
For the automatic printing option, the printer will be selected according to the following logic:
 if there is a printer code/operator record in the Detail Block of DOCU for the 
operator who marks the load as complete in SELO, the load plan sheet will 
print on that printer
 if there is no printer code/operator record in the Detail Block of DOCU for the 
operator who marks the load as complete in SELO, the load plan sheet will 
print on the printer designated in the Header Block of DOCU 
ATTRIBUTE 
TYPE OPTIONS

OUTBOUND LOAD BUILDING
Setting Up Outbound Load Building
322 0 = Do not allow pallets to be stacked
1 = Allow full pallets to be stacked
2 = Allow full pallets to be stacked and UI splits
If you select 0, pallets cannot be stacked on a truck or trailer. If you select 1 
(Allow full pallets to be stacked but no UI splits), full pallets can be stacked but 
operators cannot split pallets into two in OLOP. If you select 2 (Allow full pallets 
to be stacked and UI splits), full pallets can be stacked and operators can split 
pallets into two in OLOP.
NOTE Option 2 (Allow full pallets to be stacked and UI splits) overrides attribute 301 (Blind count not required/required). That means that the operator will 
always be required to enter the quantity in OLOP regardless of the option 
selected for attribute 301.
323 0 = Does not display quantity on load sheet
1 = Display quantity on load sheet
If you select 0, no quantities will be shown on the load sheet. If you select 1, 
quantities will be shown on the load sheet for each order. 
324 0 = Requires RF loading process
1 = RF loading process not required
If you select 0, loads must be processed in OLOP before they can be marked as 
complete. If you select 1, you can bypass processing in OLOP and work exclusively in SELO.
The following conditions apply to RF loading process not required:
 The load is considered to be complete when its status is “Ready to load”. At 
this point, the Update Status command is not available and the load’s status 
cannot be advanced any further.
 You must enter a carrier and consignee in SELO.
325 0 = Carrier, consignee not required
1 = Carrier, consignee required
If you select 0, the carrier and consignee are not required for a load in SELO. If 
you select 1, carrier and consignee information is mandatory in SELO.
Option 1 is designed for multi-leg trips in which the freight is transported first to a 
freight pool point or distribution center; thus the load’s carrier and consignee differs from the carriers and consignees on individual orders that are used to 
deliver the freight to its final destination.
ATTRIBUTE 
TYPE OPTIONS

OUTBOUND LOAD BUILDING
Setting Up Outbound Load Building
OPERATIONS 2 GUIDE 4.2* 329
326 0 = Pool shipment not required
1 = Pool shipment required
If you select 0, the Pool Carrier and Pool Consignee fields are not available in 
SELO. If you select 1, the Pool Carrier and Pool Consignee fields are available 
in SELO but not mandatory.
Option 1 is designed for multi-leg trips in which the freight is transported first to a 
freight pool point or distribution center; thus the load’s carrier and consignee differs from the carriers and consignees on individual orders that are used to 
deliver the freight to its final destination.
327 0 = OPID’s/UI’s loading (full order shipping)
1 = Carton loading (allow partial order shipping)
2 = Allow partial order based on order restrictions
If you select 0, an order cannot be split between two or more loads. If you select 
1, the consignee and carrier must match for all orders on a load. If you select 2, 
split loads are activated. With split loads, no match of either consignee or carrier 
is required for the orders on a load.
328 0 = Attach the Door in SELO only
1 = Attach the Door in SELO or OLOP (OLOP ignores 325)
If you select 0, doors can only be assigned to loads in SELO. If you select 1, 
doors can be assigned to loads in either SELO or OLOP.
329 0 = Regular bar code for door code
1 = Attach _ (underscore) as first character of door bar code
2 = Attach # (number sign) as first character of door bar code
3 = Attach | (pipe) as first character of door bar code
4 = Attach $ (dollar sign) as first character of door bar code
If you select 0, the entire bar code must be numeric. If you select 1, 2, 3 or 4, the 
first character of the bar code can be a special non-numeric character.
330 0 = Show load sheet weight in LBS
1 = Show load sheet weight in KGS
If you select 0, the weight in the SELO load sheet will be shown in pounds. If you 
select 1, the weight in the SELO load sheet will be shown in kilograms. This 
option is only available if warehouse attribute 323 is set to 1 (Display quantity on 
load sheet).
ATTRIBUTE 
TYPE OPTIONS

OUTBOUND LOAD BUILDING
Setting Up Outbound Load Building
331 0 = Ignore order group validation
1 = Order group validation required
If you select 0, order group validation will be deactivated. If you select 1, order 
group validation will be activated. Order group validation allows you to define 
two or more customers as an order group; customers assigned to an order 
group cannot be split into two or more stops in SELO.
332 0 = Do not capture photo when closing load
1 = Capture photo when closing load*
* custom use only
333 0 = Do not restrict merge to same group, stop and load in RFMG
1 = Restrict merge to same group, stop and load in RFMG
If you select 0, pallet merges in RFMG are not restricted to the same group, stop 
and load. If you select 1, pallet merges in RFMG are only allowed if all product is 
assigned to the same group, stop and load in SELO.
334 0 = Multiple Pallets Loading to Door
1 = Multiple Pallets Loading to MHE then to Door*
If you select 0, pallets are loaded directly into the truck without the use of a forklift or other material handling equipment. If you select 1, pallets are first loaded 
onto a forklift and then onto a truck.
* Only available if warehouse attribute 327 = 0 or 2
335 0 = Display Pallet Picklist in OLOP (F3 = PK)
1 = Display Load Picklist in OLOP (F3 = PL)
If you select 0, the pallet or OPID picklist will be available in OLOP showing all 
inventory levels, the OPID, quantity and from location. If you select 1, the load 
picklist will be available in OLOP. The load picklist option is recommended for 
blind loading; that is, the RF operator does not know the product to be loaded.
ATTRIBUTE 
TYPE OPTIONS

OUTBOUND LOAD BUILDING
Setting Up Outbound Load Building
OPERATIONS 2 GUIDE 4.2* 331
336 0 = Standard Trailer Loading: Left, Center, Right
1 = Two-Column Trailer Loading: Left, Right
2 = T-Block Trailer Loading: Left, Right (1 row), Center (next row)
3 = S-Type Two-Column Trailer Loading: Left-Right, Right-Left
These trailer loading options define the default pallet positions in OLOP, which 
the RF operator can override.
337 0 = Regular Loading
1 = Loading by Consignee (Store)*
* custom use only

TYPE OPTIONS
Two-Column Trailer Loading
T-Block Trailer Loading

SETTING UP YOUR PRINT RESTRICTIONS FOR LOAD DOCUMENTS
Refer to the Core Documents Guide for further information on the Print Restrictions on Load Documents field 
in DOCU.
Setting Up a Load in SELO
When setting up a load, you define the door, the building (if required), the warehouse attributes, the orders on 
the load and the stop sequence. The only mandatory entry during load creation is the code representing the 
warehouse attributes; the other essential components of a load such as the door and building, the orders and 
the stops can be added either at load creation or later when this information is known.
If carrier and consignee entry is defined as optional in WAPC, you can specify a carrier and consignee for the 
load. If carrier and consignee entry is defined as mandatory in WAPC, this information must be entered 
before you can update the load’s status to “Ready to load”.
You enter carrier and consignee information in SELO when you are working with multi-leg trips in which the 
freight is transported first to a freight pool point or distribution center. The carriers and consignees attached to 
the orders, on the other hand, are used to deliver the freight to its final destination.
You can set up your load at any flow from ENOR on.
FIELD DESCRIPTIONS (CREATE MODE)
Load This number is system generated.
External Load Press Enter to bypass this field.
Door The door assigned to the load.
Building The door’s building.
Warehouse Attributes The load’s warehouse attribute profile code.
Carrier Only enterable if warehouse attribute 325 = 1
The load’s carrier. The load’s carrier may differ from the order’s carrier if the 
load is part of a multi-leg trip in which the freight is delivered first to a freight 
pool point or distribution center.

OUTBOUND LOAD BUILDING
Setting Up a Load in SELO
OPERATIONS 2 GUIDE 4.2* 335
Consignee Only enterable if warehouse attribute 325 = 1
The load’s consignee. The load’s consignee may differ from the order’s consignee if the load is part of a multi-leg trip in which the freight is delivered first 
to a freight pool point or distribution center.
Trailer Checklist - Start
(defined in MRFP)
Optional
The trailer checklist for the start of loading. Checklists are set up in MCHK (RF 
Checklist).
Completion
(defined in MRFP)
Optional
The trailer checklist for the end of loading. 
Carrier Probill Number Optional
The load’s carrier probill number.
Weight Optional
The maximum weight for the load. If warehouse attribute 338 = 1, the RF 
operator will not be allowed to continue loading in OLOP if the maximum 
weight is exceeded.
Cube Optional
The maximum cube for the load. This field is for display purposes only and is 
not enforced in OLOP.
Disable IPRO Validation N = No
Y = Yes 
If you select No or leave blank, normal IPRO validation will occur in OLOP. If 
you select Yes, normal IPRO validation will be deactivated in OLOP.
FIELD DESCRIPTIONS (CREATE MODE)

NOTE The carrier in SELO corresponds to the carrier information in the Carrier 
Details Block of ENOR. That is, the driver code, driver name, power unit number, 
carry unit number, vessel, voyage, seal number 1/2 and temperatures (front, middle, 
back, etc.). It does NOT correspond to the carrier in the Header Block of ENOR. This 
carrier is entered and modified in ENOR only. You cannot change this carrier in 
SELO.

DELETING A LOAD
When you delete a load, all orders on the load are released and available to be assigned to another load. If a 
load has not been loaded (that is, placed in the truck), you can delete the load in SELO. If a load has been 
loaded, you must unload it first in OLOP and then delete it in SELO.

SUSPENDING AND RELEASING A LOAD
You can suspend a load if it has a status of “Ready to Load” or “Loading”. No activity is allowed on a 
suspended load in either SELO or OLOP until the load is released and orders attached to a suspended load 
cannot be confirmed. You suspend a load at a door when you want to perform an emergency loading or 
unloading of another truck at the same door.
1 Enter SELO.
2 Key in your load number and press Enter.

4 When prompted to proceed with the update, click on Yes.
5 Select your current building for the load from the pick list.

OPERATIONS 2 GUIDE 4.2* 345
Loading Orders in OLOP
You must load the orders in reverse stop sequence as defined in SELO. For example, if you have stops 1, 2 
and 3, you must load stop 3 first, then stop 2 and lastly stop 1. If you have multiple orders assigned to the 
same stop, you can load those orders in any sequence. 
Before you can load orders, orders on the load must be picked and the load’s status must be set to “Ready to 
load”. As well, the load cannot be locked. A load is considered locked under the following circumstances:
 an operator has accessed the load in SELO
 an operator has accessed an order on the load in ENOR
 an operator has accessed an order on the load in CHOF 
1 Enter OLOP.

OLOP screen showing prompt for load number
2 Key in your load number or external load number and press Enter. If the load is valid, AccellosOne 3PL 
will display the door code, building code and stop number. The stop number shown will be the next stop 
to be loaded.
NOTE OLOP is governed by the operator restrictions set up in RFOT (RF Operator 
Task Assign).

SPLITTING PALLETS IN OLOP
You can split a full pallet into two or more half pallets if the pallet was picked in normal or bulk picking mode in 
RFPIC. Pallet splitting in OLOP is not available in the following circumstances:
 the pallet was picked in case pick mode in RFPIC
 the pallet was picked in normal or bulk picking mode in RFPIC, but the UI has been assigned process 
values such as catch weight or serial number
Pallet splitting must be activated in WAPC (Warehouse Attribute Profile Code).
1 Enter OLOP.
2 If you load by load number, key in your load or external load number and press Enter. If you load by door, 
key in your door code and press Enter. If required, key in your building code and press Enter.
3 Enter or scan in your UI value.

OLOP screen showing prompt to confirm PID position
4 If the system-generated pallet position is correct, press Enter to confirm it. If you wish to enter your own 
pallet position, key in a new value and press Enter.

accept the system-generated pallet position or key in your own pallet position and press Enter. Lastly, 
press Enter to accept the remaining quantity on the pallet
8 Continue to process the load normally.
Unloading Orders in OLOP
When you unload orders in OLOP, the product is automatically moved to the door’s staging location. 
Unloaded orders can be reloaded onto the original load in OLOP or can be assigned to another load by 
deleting the order from the load in SELO and then adding it to a new load.
The following rules apply to unloading:
 the load must have a status of “Loading” in SELO
 orders must be unloaded in stop sequence order; for example, first stop 1, then stop 2 and lastly stop 3
 you must enter unload mode before scanning in the UI value or OPID
1 Enter OLOP.
2 Key in your load number and press Enter.

quantity of the pallet.
a) Key in the quantity remaining on 
the pallet and press Enter.
b) Press Enter again to confirm the 
split quantity.

PID, up to four inventory levels, the order number, line number, ship date and load status for each outbound 
PID reported on.

OUTBOUND LOAD BUILDING
Confirming the Load
OPERATIONS 2 GUIDE 4.2* 351
Confirming the Load
The load must be fully loaded in OLOP before you can confirm it. You confirm a load in two steps: first you set 
the load’s status to complete in SELO (not required if you have selected the warehouse attribute “Advance 
the complete status of a load automatically”) and then you confirm the load in CHOF.
1 If the load’s status is automatically advanced to completed after loading, proceed to step 6.
2 Enter SELO.
3 Key in the load number that you wish to confirm and press Enter.

Set Up Load (SELO) screen showing load with a status of “Loading”
4 Click on Update Status . The status will change to “Complete”.
5 Click on Exit to exit SELO.
6 Enter CHOF.
7 Press Enter the required number of times to position your cursor in either the Load Number or External 
Load Number fields.
8 Key in your load number and press Enter.
9 If required, advance the flow of the load to COOR (Confirm Order) and print any mandatory order documents.

OUTBOUND LOAD BUILDING
Printing the Load Plan Sheet

Time Stamp and Confirm Orders (CHOF) screen
10 When you reach COOR, press Enter to position your cursor in the Ship Date field. Press Enter to accept 
the current date as your ship date or key in a new date and press Enter.
11 Click on Select Flow to confirm the load. Confirming the load will automatically confirm all orders on the 
load as well.
12 Click on Exit to exit.
Printing the Load Plan Sheet
The load plan sheet shows the pallet position, UI or OPID value, up to four inventory levels and the number of 
units. Pallet positions — LS001/LN001 (left stacked/left non-stacked), CS001/CN001 (Center stacked/center 
non-stacked), RS001/RN001 (right stacked/right non-stacked), LS002/LN002 (left stacked/left non-stacked), 
etc. — are defined by the operator in OLOP.
You can print the load plan sheet in either SELO or LOLD.

Looking Up Loads in LOLOAD
Looking Up Loads in LOLOAD
LOLOAD shows each order line on the load including the load status, stop number, door location and door 
warehouse. You can query by door and building, load number, load status, order number and stop number.
LOLOAD supports the following load statuses: 2 (Ready to Load), 3 (Loading) and 4 (Complete).
1 Enter LOLOAD.
2 Press Enter until your cursor is positioned in the field that you wish to query on.
3 Key in your search criteria and press F2.

LOLOAD screen showing three order lines
4 Use your arrow keys to scroll through each order line on the load. 
5 When you finish looking up your load, press F4 to exit.
Looking Up Load Details in LOLD
This look-up program shows the load number/external load number, order number, flow, to ship date, gross 
and net weight, quantity, stop number, door, building and load status. If you click on Order Detail, you can see 
the order quantity, ship quantity, gross and net weight, the outbound pallet ID and up to four inventory levels. 
If you click on Load Carrier, you can see information from the Carrier Block such as the driver code, name, 
power unit number, carry unit number, seal numbers, etc.
You can access LOLD from the Main Menu as a stand-alone program or you can access LOLD from SELO.

pallet ID and load status.

a) Enter LOLD. a) Key in your load number and 
press Enter.
b) Click on Enter Query .

PALLET CONTROL
Overview
Overview
The pallet control system allows you to track pallets and other equipment belonging to any AccellosOne 3PL 
account — that is, any customer, carrier, consignee and shipper. For each pallet that you ship or receive, you 
can track its type, its ownership and the number shipped or received.
If you wish to track your own pallets, you must set up a house account in CUST. Your account type in CUST 
for your house account must be I for Invoice Only. House accounts must be activated by HighJump before 
you can use them on receipts and orders. 
You can enter your pallet details during receipt entry, receipt confirmation, order entry, order confirmation or 
any combination of these flows. You can also make adjustments to your pallet details in an adjustment 
program when the change that you are making does not involve a receipt or order.
Setting Up the Pallet Control System
There are two steps involved in setting up the pallet control system:
 you define your account restrictions in COMP (Company Parameters Block)
 you set up your pallet types in PALL
DEFINING YOUR ACCOUNT RESTRICTIONS IN COMP
The Activate Pallet Restrictions flag in COMP determines whether or not you are restricted to specific 
accounts while working in the Pallet Details Block of ENRE and ENOR. For example, if this flag is set to Yes 
and you are working in ENOR, you can only receive pallets from and ship pallets to active accounts on the 
order (that is, the customer, consignee, sold-to or carrier). If this flag is set to No and you are working in 
ENOR, you can receive and ship pallets to any active account in your company.
COMP screen showing Pallet Restrictions Activated flag set to Yes

PALLET CONTROL
Setting Up the Pallet Control System
OPERATIONS 2 GUIDE 4.2* 361
SETTING UP PALLET TYPES IN PALL
You set up your pallet types in PALL (Pallet Types). A pallet type can be a type of pallet or any other piece of 
equipment belonging to customers, carriers, consignees and shippers with whom you deal on a regular basis. 
For example, you can track totes or blankets as “pallet types” in PALL.
1 Enter PALL.

Pallet Types (PALL)
2 Click on Create Record.
3 Key in your pallet code and press Enter.
4 Key in a meaningful description for your pallet code and press Enter.
5 Use your pick list to select the unit of measure that you wish to use to measure the size of your pallets.
6 Select the appropriate pallet type from the dropdown list.
7 If required, key in the length of the pallet or piece of equipment and press Enter. If you do not need to 
record the length, press Enter to bypass this field.
8 If required, key in the width of the pallet or piece of equipment and press Enter. If you do not need to 
record the width, press Enter to bypass this field.
9 If required, key in the height of the pallet or piece of equipment and press Enter. If you do not need to 
record the height, press Enter to bypass this field.
If you enter the length, width and height, the system will calculate the cube of the pallet or piece of equipment automatically.
TIP If you receive damaged or substandard pallets, you should set up special pallet 
types such as DMGE or CHEF-BAD to track these pallets. If you do not set up special 
types for damaged or substandard pallets, you will be unable to differentiate the different grades of pallets that you receive.

PALLET CONTROL
Shipping and Receiving Pallets
10 If required, use your pick list to select the unit of measure that you wish to use to measure the weight of 
your pallets. Then key in the weight and press Enter.
11 Click on Return to Main to exit create record mode.

Pallet Types (PALL) showing CHEP pallet
12 Click on Exit to exit.
Shipping and Receiving Pallets
You ship and receive pallets in ENRE or ENOR by entering the appropriate value in the Pallet Details field. 
There are three values that you can enter in this field:
 E for Entry
 C for Confirmation
 B for Both
If you select Entry, you will be prompted to enter your pallet details when you enter your receipt in ENRE or 
when you enter your order in ENOR. If you select Confirmation, you will be prompted to enter your pallet 
details in CHRF (Time Stamp and Confirm Receipt) for a receipt or in CHOF (Time Stamp and Confirm 

PALLET CONTROL
Shipping and Receiving Pallets
OPERATIONS 2 GUIDE 4.2* 363
Orders) for an order. If you select Both, you will be prompted to enter your pallet details twice: once during 
receipt/order entry and once during receipt/order confirmation.
SHIPPING AND RECEIVING PALLETS IN ENRE
1 Enter ENRE.
2 Enter your customer, shipper, bill to information, etc. normally.

Enter Receipts (ENRE)
3 In the Pallet Details field, key in the appropriate value (E for Entry, C for Confirmation or B for Both) and 
press Enter.
4 Enter the remaining fields in the Header Block of ENRE in the normal manner.
5 If you entered either E for Entry or B for Both, the Pallet Block will appear.
TIP Pallet transactions are separate transactions that are unrelated to the product 
received on a receipt or shipped on an order. For example, you can receive product in 
and ship pallets out on the same receipt and you can ship product out and receive 
pallets in on the same order.

for Received or S for Shipped). Received means that you are receiving pallets from the account that you 
specified in the previous field. Shipped means that you are shipping pallets to the account that you specified in the previous field.

10 If required, key in a remark for the pallet transaction in the Reference field and press Enter. If you do not 
require a remark, press Enter with the Reference field blank to add the detail line to the Pallet Block.

PERFORMING AN EVEN EXCHANGE OF PALLETS IN ENRE/ENOR
You use the even exchange function when you are performing a straight or even exchange between yourself 
(that is, the warehouse) and an account; that is, the number of inbound pallets is the same as the number of 
outbound pallets. 
For example, suppose you receive product from Customer A and Customer A’s product is on the carrier’s 
pallets of which there are ten. Rather than unloading the product from the carrier’s pallets so that you can 
return the pallets, you give the carrier an equivalent number of your own pallets — that is, ten. When you 
exchange pallets, there is no change to your pallet balances and the total in the Balance Block in LOPC is 
zero. 
An even exchange of pallets is always performed as a single transaction. For example, if you exchange 
pallets in ENRE, the transaction is considered to be complete once you enter or confirm the receipt. There is 
no need to “ship” the pallets in ENOR.
1 Enter ENRE or ENOR.
2 Enter your receipt or order.

PALLET CONTROL
Shipping and Receiving Pallets
OPERATIONS 2 GUIDE 4.2* 369
3 In the Pallet Details field, key in the appropriate value (E for Entry, C for Confirmation or B for Both) and 
press Enter.

Pallet Block in ENOR
4 Key in your account code and press Enter or use your pick list to select it. The account is the party (customer, shipper, consignee or carrier) with whom you are exchanging.
5 In the Type field, key in E for Exchanged and press Enter.
6 Key in your pallet code and press Enter or use your pick list to select it.
7 Key in the number of pallets that you are exchanging and press Enter.
8 If required, key in a remark for the exchange in the Reference field and press Enter. If you do not require 
a remark, press Enter to position your cursor on the next line.

PALLET CONTROL

11 Click on Exit to exit to the Line Block.
12 Enter your Line Block information and continue to process the receipt or order normally.
PERFORMING AN UNEVEN EXCHANGE OF PALLETS IN ENRE/ENOR
An uneven exchange is an exchange in which the number of incoming pallets does not match the number of 
outgoing pallets; for example, you receive ten pallets but you ship eight pallets. An uneven exchange requires 
two lines in the Pallet Block: an R line for the incoming pallets and an S line for the outgoing pallets.
In the example given in the previous paragraph, you would enter an R line for ten pallets and an S line for 
eight pallets.
WORKING WITH HOUSE ACCOUNTS
If you are tracking your own pallets through a house account, you must enter two lines in the Pallet Details 
Block of ENRE/ENOR: one line for the house account and one line for the regular account.

PALLET CONTROL
Looking Up Pallet Information
OPERATIONS 2 GUIDE 4.2* 375

Time Stamp and Confirm Receipt (CHRF) screen showing Pallet Block for receipt 1321
Looking Up Pallet Information
There are two look-up programs for pallets:
 LOPC (Look Up Pallet Control)
This program shows your pallet transactions and balances for each account.
 LORE/LOOR (Look Up Receipts/Look Up Orders)
The Pallet Details flag in these programs show the value that you entered in the Pallet Details field in ENRE/
ENOR for a given receipt or order.
LOOKING UP YOUR PALLET TRANSACTIONS AND BALANCES IN LOPC
You look up your pallet accounts in LOPC (Look Up Pallet Control). There are two tabs in this program: the 
Balances tab and the History tab. The Balances tab shows the total number of pallets by pallet type and 
confirmation status; for example, if you have ten pallets of type X whose status is confirmed and five pallets of 
the same type whose status is unconfirmed, LOPC will show two lines in the Balance Block. 
If the total is a positive number (for example, 10), it means that you owe that account ten pallets; that is, you 
have ten pallets belonging to that account. If the total is a negative number (for example, -5), it means that 
account owes you five pallets; that is, that account has five of your pallets. If the total is zero, it indicates a 
straight exchange; for example, you gave the account four pallets and the account gave you four pallets.

PALLET CONTROL
Looking Up Pallet Information
If you are tracking your own pallets through a house account, a positive number indicates pallets in inventory 
belonging to you that have not been lent to another account. A negative number, on the other hand, indicates 
pallets belonging to you that have been lent to another account and are owed to you.
The History tab shows each pallet transaction that has taken place for a given account.

If the pallet transaction is on an unconfirmed receipt or order, the status will be 
N. If the pallet transaction was made as an adjustment in ADPC, the status will 
be Y.

R = Receipt
A = Adjustment
Conf Y = Yes
N = No
If the pallet transaction is on a confirmed receipt or order, the status will be Y. 
If the pallet transaction is on an unconfirmed receipt or order, the status will be 
N. If the pallet transaction was made as an adjustment in ADPC, the status will 
be Y.

If you wish to make an adjustment to your pallet quantities without entering a receipt or an order, you use the 
program ADPC (Adjust Pallet Control). This program allows you to add pallets to or remove pallets from any 
account on your system. When you look up an adjustment in LOPC (Look Up Pallet Control), the value in the 
Confirmed field will always be Y for Yes.

transaction for the house account and one transaction for the regular account.

ITEM PROCESS VALUES
Overview
Overview
Item process values allow you to create predefined messages and capture operator-entered values that can 
be attached to a particular item during order or receipt entry. You can choose to print these messages and 
values on the inbound or outbound document that you specify or you can store them on the system only. 
You use item process values when you wish to:
 capture item-specific information such as serial numbers, temperatures, catch weights or purchase 
order numbers for the purpose of printing them on an inbound or outbound document such as a bill of 
lading, label or other document 
 attach a standard predefined message to a document with no value to be captured (for example, 
“REPACK ITEM TO COMPANY X’S SPECS”)1
If you are capturing a value, you can specify whether you wish to store the value in inventory; if you store the 
value in inventory, you can look it up in LOIP (Look Up Item Process). If you do not store the value in 
inventory, it will be automatically deleted when you confirm the order or receipt or when you purge the order 
or receipt in PURG. 
PROCESS VALUES VS. INVENTORY LEVELS
You use item process values when you wish to attach a message or operator-entered value to a particular 
item or to a small group of items during order or receipt entry. If you wished to attach a message to all items 
belonging to a customer, you would use the programs MESS, DEME and DPME. 
If you wished to record and track serial numbers for all of a customer’s items, you would set up an inventory 
level in DILP. If you used item process values to record serial numbers instead of an inventory level, you 
could not assign a location to a particular serial number and no billing or allocation by serial number would be 
possible.
Setting Up Your Item Process Code in IPRO
In this program, you set up your item process codes. Item process codes are attached to your item process 
profile (IPRP).
1. only available for documents that support item process values.
FIELD DESCRIPTIONS
Process Code Mandatory
Your item process code; for example, CW for Catch Weight.

ITEM PROCESS VALUES
Setting Up Your Item Process Code in IPRO
OPERATIONS 2 GUIDE 4.2* 385
Description Mandatory
Your process code description
Type of Value CUBE = Cube
HGT = Height
LEN = Length
LEV2 = Inventory Level 2
OTHR = Other
SER = Serial Number in Weight Bar Code
SN = Serial Number
TARE = Tare Weight
TEMP = Temperature
WGTG = Gross Weight
WGTN = Net Weight
WID = Width
The type of value that you are capturing. If you are capturing a value during 
receipt or order entry, you can use any of the above types. If you are setting 
up a standard predefined message, use OTHR as your type of value.
Entry Value Y = Yes
N = No
If you set Entry to Yes, you can go to the Process Block and enter a value during order or receipt entry. If you set Entry to No, the predefined text will print 
on the document that you specify in the Detail Block and you can, if required, 
record the value manually on this document.
Inbound / Outbound / 
Both
Only available if you are capturing a value
I = Inbound
O = Outbound
B = Both
If you specify I for Inbound, you are capturing a value during an inbound process. If you specify O for Outbound, you are capturing a value during an outbound process. If you specify B for Both, you are capturing a value during both 
an inbound and outbound process.
FIELD DESCRIPTIONS

ITEM PROCESS VALUES
Setting Up Your Item Process Code in IPRO
Per SKU Class
(defined in SKCL)
Only available if you are capturing a value
The SKU class to which you wish to attach the message or value. For example, if you specify Pallets, you will be prompted to enter a value for each 
inbound or outbound pallet or for any other SKU type assigned to the SKU 
class of Pallets. If you specify Cases and the like, you will be prompted to 
enter a value for each inbound or outbound case or any other SKU type 
assigned to the class Cases and the like.
Use SKU class 6 (Other) when you wish to capture one value for each receipt/
order line rather one value based on the line quantity. For example, you want 
to capture one temperature or inbound dimension for each receipt line regardless of the line quantity.
Entry Length Only available if you are capturing a value
The length of the value to be entered. The maximum length permitted is 250 
characters. If the item that IPRO is attached to is also attached to a SCPR 
(Scan Parameter Profile) record, the length in SCPR will override the length in 
IPRO.
Data Type Only available if you are capturing a value
CHAR = Character
DATE = Date
INT = Integer
MONY = Money
NUM = Number
This field controls the formatting of values that you capture. If you select 
DATE, the number that you enter will be formatted as a date. If you select 
MONY, the number that you enter will be formatted in dollars and cents (for 
example, $99.99).
Use CHAR for serial and purchase order numbers.
FIELD DESCRIPTIONS

ITEM PROCESS VALUES
Setting Up Your Item Process Code in IPRO
OPERATIONS 2 GUIDE 4.2* 387
Transfer Value to Inventory
Only available if you are capturing a value
Y = Yes
N = No
If you specify Yes, the following will occur:
 item process code values will be transferred to inventory when the order or 
receipt is confirmed and can be viewed in LOIP (Look Up Item Process)
 inbound process values, if any, will be transferred to outbound orders when 
you run ORPE (Order Process Entry) or RFOPS (RF Outbound Process 
Scanning)
If you specify No, there will be no transfer of item process code values to 
inventory and therefore you cannot view them in LOIP.
Create Automatic 
Records
Only available if you are capturing a value
Y = Yes
N = No
If you specify Yes, you can create one process code value per pallet (if you 
specify Pallets in the Per SKU Class field) or one process code value per 
case/carton (if you specify Cases and the like in the Per SKU Class field). If 
you specify No, no records will be created for each pallet or each case/carton 
and you will not be able to access the Process Block of ENRE/ENOR.
The No option is only available if you are capturing temperatures by means of 
the TEMP code or if you use RF to receive or ship product.
FIELD DESCRIPTIONS

ITEM PROCESS VALUES
Setting Up Your Item Process Code in IPRO
Entry Validation Rules Only available if you are capturing a non-numeric value and there is no SCPR 
record attached to the item
Equal or greater
Equal or less
Match exactly
No validation
If you specify Equal or greater, the value that you capture can have a length 
greater than or equal to the value in the Entry Length field. If you specify Equal 
or less, the value that you capture can have a length less than or equal to the 
value in the Entry Length field.
If you specify Match Exactly, the length of the value that you capture must 
match the value that you enter in the Entry Length field. If you specify No validation, no validation will be performed on the entry length.
Allow Duplicates Only available if you are capturing a value
Y = Yes
N = No
If you specify Yes, duplicate entries within the same receipt or order line will 
be allowed. If you specify No, no duplicates will be allowed within the same 
receipt or order line.
Delete After Confirmation Only available if you are capturing a value
Y = Yes
N = No
If you specify Yes, the system will delete the item process code entries from 
the receipt or order once you confirm it. If you specify No, the item process 
code entries will remain on your system until you purge your orders and 
receipts in PURG.
FIELD DESCRIPTIONS

ITEM PROCESS VALUES
Setting Up Your Item Process Code in IPRO
OPERATIONS 2 GUIDE 4.2* 389
Mandatory Entry Only available if you are capturing a value
Y = Yes
N = No
If you specify Yes, you will not be able to confirm the receipt or order if all process values have not been entered. If you specify No, you can confirm the 
receipt or order even if there are missing process values.
Locate Weight in Bar 
Code Dynamically

From This SKU Class

you are capturing weights from two or more SKU classes

N = No

order line 1 = 5 cases
order line 2 = 25 cases (1 full pallet)
order line 3 = 30 cases ( 1 full pallet plus 5 cases)

Y = Yes

override the standard weight measure code defined in IPRO.

Setting Up Your Item Process Profile in IPRP
In this program, you attach the item process code or codes that you created in IPRO to the item process 
profile. In ITEM you will attach this profile to the item to which it belongs. 
If you set the Transfer Value to Inventory flag in IPRO to Yes, you can assign dependencies to item process 
codes. You use dependencies when you are capturing weight or cube information and you want to attach this 
weight or cube process code to another process code.
For example, you set up a process code of BC to capture a bar coded label and assign this process code the 
dependency of Parent. Then you set up a second process code to capture the weight or cube that is encoded 
in the bar code label and assign this process code the dependency of Child. When you look up the item in 
LOIP (Look Up Item Process), the parent process code will appear in the header block of this program and 
the child process code of codes will appear in the detail block.

ITEM PROCESS VALUES
Setting Up Your Item Process Profile in IPRP
OPERATIONS 2 GUIDE 4.2* 393
You do not use dependencies when you are attaching a predefined message to a document or when you are 
capturing values like serial numbers or catch weights and these values do not contain weight or cube information that form part of another item process code. 
1 Enter IPRP.
2 If you need a new item process profile, click on Create Record.
3 Key in a process profile code and press Enter.
4 Key in a description for your code and press Enter.
5 In the Process Code field, key in your process code and press Enter or use your pick list function to 
select it. To select a code using a pick list, press F10 to display the pick list and click on Execute Query to 
display the list of codes. Then position your cursor over the appropriate code using your arrow keys and 
click on Select to select it.
FIELD DESCRIPTIONS
Process Profile Code Mandatory
Your process profile code; for example, PO for Purchase Order Number.
Description Mandatory
Your process profile code description.
Process Code
(defined in IPRO)
Mandatory
Your item process code.
Dependency Only available if you set the Transfer Value to Inventory flag in IPRO to Yes.
P = Parent Code
C = Child Code
N = No Dependency (default)
The parent and child dependencies are only used when you have bar coded 
process codes containing weight or cube information. The bar coded process 
code is the parent code and the weight or cube values are the child codes. 
Item process codes assigned the dependency of child must have CUBE, 
WGTG or WGTN as their Type Description in IPRO.
Process values set up as parent codes will be appear in header block of LOIP. 
Process values set up as child codes will appear in the detail block of LOIP. 
You cannot query on child process values.

Setting Up Catch Weights

Depositor Workflow Profile (DIFP) screen showing flow CAWE (Capture Weight)
Setting Up Catch Weights
The entry of catch weights can be either mandatory or optional. If you define catch weights as optional, 
you will be able to confirm the receipt or order without entering all your catch weights. If you define catch 
weights as mandatory, you will not be able to advance to the next flow or confirm the receipt or order unless 
all your catch weights have been entered.
You define catch weights as mandatory by attaching the appropriate special verify program to your DIFP 
profile. There are two special verify programs:
 CREW (Check receipt weights from IPRO) for receipts
 CORW (Check order weights from IPRO) for orders
For inbound catch weights, the following setup is required:
FLOW
SET IN SPECIAL VERIFICATION 
BLOCK OF DIFP
for any flow except CORE (Confirm 
Receipt)
Complete flag = Yes
Sequence flag = B (Before) or A (After)
for CORE (Confirm Receipt) Complete flag = Yes
Sequence flag = B (Before)

ITEM PROCESS VALUES
Setting Up Catch Weights
OPERATIONS 2 GUIDE 4.2* 397
For outbound catch weights, the following setup is required:
You can attach these special verify programs to any flow in DIFP. If you attach your special verify to CORE 
(Confirm Receipt) or COOR (Confirm Order), you will not be able to confirm the receipt or order until all your 
catch weights have been entered. If you attach your special verify to any other flow, you will be unable to 
advance to the next flow until all your catch weights have been entered.
There are two catch weight options: you can enter catch weights for reference purposes only or you can enter 
catch weights that override the system-defined gross or net weight of each receipt/order line.
FLOW

for any flow except COOR (Confirm Order) Complete flag = Yes
Sequence flag = B (Before) or A (After)
for COOR (Confirm Order) Complete flag = Yes
Sequence flag = B (Before)

of ENRE/ENOR, but does not change the 
gross or net weight of the receipt/order line.

for Standard Weight.

Adding Process Values to a Receipt

DIFP screen showing special verification program CORW (Check Order Weights from IPRO)
Adding Process Values to a Receipt
You add process values to a receipt by entering the Process Value Block of ENRE.
1 Enter ENRE.
2 Enter the Header Block information for the receipt.
3 In the Line Block, enter your item code and any required inventory levels.
4 Enter your expected and received quantities.

Looking Up Process Values in LOIP
You can look up item process values in LOIP (Look Up Item Process) if the Transfer Value to Inventory flag of 
the process code has been set to Y for Yes and if the order or receipt has been confirmed. You can query on 
an item process code or on the value itself. For each code or value that you look up, LOIP shows the 
following:
 the date that the product was processed
 the flow at which the item entered inventory (CR = Confirmed Receipt or CO = Confirmed Order)
 the receipt or order number on which it was entered
 the line number in the receipt or order
 the line number in the Location Block
 the process quantity
 the gross weight, net weight or cube (only available for parent child dependencies defined in IPRP in 
which the Type Description of the child is set to CUBE, WGTG or WGTN)
1 Enter LOIP.

2 Key in your criteria and press Enter. You can query on customer code, level 1, 2, 3 and 4 value, process 
value or process code.

you would enter your value (for example, A-1234 for a serial number query).

Modifying Process Values in ENRE/ENOR
You can modify an item process value at any flow before CORE (Confirm Receipt) or COOR (Confirm Order).

Modifying Process Values in ENRE/ENOR
TRANSFERRING PROCESS VALUES
You can automatically transfer inbound process values such as catch weights and serial numbers to 
outbound orders if you set the Transfer Value to Inventory flag in IPRO to Y for Yes. For example, you receive 
item A, lot 101, pallet ID 123 and assign it a catch weight of 100 lbs. When you ship the same inventory in 
ENOR and run the program ORPE (Order Process Entry), AccellosOne 3PL will automatically transfer the 
catch weight of 100 lbs. to the order.
You can only transfer process values attached to full pallets or to whichever SKU type is assigned the highest 
SKU class. For example, if your quantity breakdown is 100 cases per pallet and you receive a partial pallet 
(say, 75 cases), you cannot transfer process values.
The process values can be attached to any SKU in the item’s quantity breakdown. For example, if your 
quantity breakdown is pallet/case/each, you can capture one process value per pallet, one process value for 
each case on the pallet or one process value for each each on the pallet.
SETTING UP THE TRANSFER OF PROCESS VALUES
The following setup is required in IPRO:
 Type of Value = the type of value being captured
 Inbound / Outbound / Both = Both
 Per SKU class = any SKU class
 Create Automatic Records = Yes
 Transfer Value to Inventory = Yes
Example 1 — Setup for Catch Weights in IPRO

IPRO screen showing process code for catch weights called CW1
Example 2 — Setup for Serial Numbers in IPRO

Performing the Transfer
OPERATIONS 2 GUIDE 4.2* 409

IPRO screen showing process code for serial numbers called SN1
Performing the Transfer
There are five steps to follow in performing the transfer:
 you enter your receipt in ENRE
 you enter your process values in REPE (Receipt Process Entry)
 you print your receipt documents and confirm the receipt in the normal manner
 you enter your order in ENOR
 you transfer your process values in ORPE (Order Process Entry)
ENTERING THE RECEIPT IN ENRE
The quantity being received must always equal one full pallet (or one drum if drum is assigned to the highest 
SKU class). For example, if you receive in cases, the number of cases that you receive must equal one full 
pallet.
ENTERING PROCESS VALUES IN REPE
You enter your process values in REPE (Receipt Process Entry). You can enter your process values at any 
flow before CORE (Confirm Receipt).
1 Enter REPE.
2 Key in your receipt number and press Enter.
3 Key in your line number and press Enter.
4 Key in your process values.

TRANSFERRING INBOUND PROCESS VALUES TO OUTBOUND ORDERS IN ORPE
You transfer your process values to the order in ORPE (Order Process Entry). You can transfer your process 
values at any flow before COOR (Confirm Order) provided that you have either run allocation or you have 
entered an R-type line in ENOR with all inventory levels specified.
The following restrictions apply to the transfer of process values:
 You cannot transfer process values if you are missing one or more values. For example, if you have five 
cases on one full pallet and one of the cases is missing a process value, you cannot transfer the process values in ORPE. You must enter the missing catch weight(s) in ENOR and then return to ORPE to 
perform the transfer.
 You cannot transfer process values if you have two or more inventory entities that are identical. For 
example, you have two pallets with the same level 1/2/3 values and you wish to ship out a single pallet.
 Unless you are clearing out all inventory, the quantity being shipped must always equal one full pallet (or 
one drum if drum is assigned to the highest SKU class). For example, if you ship in cases, the number of 
cases that you ship must equal one full pallet.

2 Key in your order number and press Enter.

QUICK RESPONSE LABELS
Overview
Overview
The Quick Response system allows you to generate and print MH10 or UCC-128 shipping labels for 
outbound product. You can generate labels for both inventory items as well as non-inventory items such as 
shipping containers, pallets, cages and other equipment used to hold product.
Quick Response labels require a custom EDI document. If you wish to print actual labels, you will also require 
a custom label document.
The unique number on a Quick Response label is made up of 20 digits:
 a two-digit prefix (supplied by manufacturer/consignee)
 a one-digit number for the shipping container type
 a seven-digit member number (supplied by manufacturer/consignee)
 a nine-digit serial number (sequentially generated by Warehouse-Logic)
 a one-digit check digit
the check
digit
the twodigit prefix
the type of
shipping
container
the sevendigit member
number the nine-digit
serial number

QUICK RESPONSE LABELS
Setting Up Quick Response Labels in QURE
OPERATIONS 2 GUIDE 4.2* 415
Setting Up Quick Response Labels in QURE
You set up your Quick Response labels in QURE (Quick Response Setup). In QURE you must specify a 
unique customer/consignee combination for each record that you set up.
In QURE you define:
 the customer and consignee
 whether Quick Response labels are mandatory
 the SKU class for your labels
 the inventory levels to be printed on the labels
 whether to allow containers within containers
 the prefix, member number and serial number for the label
 any documents that are required (must be programmed by HighJump)
If you ship both Quick Response and non-Quick Response orders to the same consignee, you must set up 
two consignee codes in CONS (Consignees): one code for your Quick Response orders and a second code 
for your non-Quick Response orders.
FIELD DESCRIPTIONS
Customer Code Mandatory
The customer that you are setting up for Quick Response labels.
Consignee Code Mandatory
The consignee that you are setting up for Quick Response labels.
Mandatory Process A = Always
O = Optional
If you select A for Always, the process is mandatory and you will be required 
to print your labels before confirming the order. If you select O for Optional, 
you will be prompted with the message “This is an optional process. Do you 
want to continue?” when printing your labels in QRLE.
If you respond to the question with a Yes, Quick Response is activated for the 
order and you can proceed to print your labels in QRLE either immediately or 
at a later time. If you respond to the question with a No, Quick Response will 
be deactivated for the order and you will not be able to access the order again 
in QRLE.

QUICK RESPONSE LABELS
Setting Up Quick Response Labels in QURE
SKU Class Mandatory
The SKU class for your Quick Response labels. All order quantities for this 
customer/consignee combination must be in SKU codes that belong to this 
SKU class. For example, if boxes and cases have been assigned different 
SKU classes in SKUS (Stock Keeping Units), you cannot have box and case 
quantities on the same order. 
Default SKU Reserved for future use.
Scan UPC Y = Yes
N = No
If you select Y for Yes, you can scan in the UPC code from a quick response 
label instead of the item code. AccellosOne 3PL will retrieve the correct item 
code from the UPC code that you scan. This feature requires a UPC code 
attached to the item in ITEM.
If you select N for No, you cannot scan in the UPC code from a quick 
response label.
Inventory Level Entered 1 = Level 1
2 = Level 2
3 = Level 3
4 = Level 4
The number of inventory levels that you can enter on your labels. For example, if you select Level 1, you will be able to enter level 1 values only on your 
labels; if you select Level 2, you will be able to enter level 1 and level 2 values 
on your labels.
Allow Container within 
Container
Y = Yes
N = No
If you select Y for Yes, you can create labels for non-AccellosOne 3PL entities 
such as pallets, cages, shipping containers, etc. For example, you create 10 
labels for 10 boxes and then put the 10 boxes on a single pallet. The Allow 
Container within Container function allows you to create a label for the pallet 
that contains information about the product on the pallet.
If you select N for No, you cannot create labels for non- AccellosOne 3PL entities used to store or hold product.
FIELD DESCRIPTIONS

QUICK RESPONSE LABELS
Setting Up Quick Response Labels in QURE
OPERATIONS 2 GUIDE 4.2* 417
Default Container Type Only available if you set the Allow Container within Container field to Y for Yes
There are three container types for Quick Response:
0 = case or carton
1 = pallet (larger than a case or carton)
2 = larger than a pallet
Serial Prefix Mandatory
A two-digit number provided by the manufacturer or consignee.
Member Number Mandatory
A seven-digit number provided by the manufacturer or consignee.
Start Mandatory
Your starting serial number (must be nine digits).
End Mandatory
Your ending serial number (must be nine digits).
Current Generated by system
Your current serial number.
QRS Document Code Optional 
Your EDI document. Must be set up by HighJump.
Label Document Code Mandatory 
Your label document. Must be set up by HighJump.
FIELD DESCRIPTIONS

QUICK RESPONSE LABELS
Setting Up Quick Response Labels in QURE
PROCEDURE
1 Enter QURE.

Quick Response Setup (QURE)
2 Key in your customer code and press Enter.
3 Key in your consignee code and press Enter.
4 In the Mandatory Process field, key in A for Always or O for Optional and press Enter.
5 In the SKU Class field, use your pick list to select your SKU class.
6 Press Enter to bypass the Default SKU field.
7 In the Scan UPC field, key in Y for Yes or N for No and press Enter.
8 In the Inventory Level Entered field, key in the appropriate inventory level and press Enter.
9 In the Allow Container within Container field, key in Y for Yes or N for No and press Enter.
10 If required, key in your default container type and press Enter.
11 In the Serial Prefix field, key in your two-digit serial number and press Enter.
12 In the Member Number field, key in your seven-digit number for the consignee and press Enter.
13 Key in your starting number and press Enter.
14 Key in your ending number and press Enter.
15 If required, key in your QRS document code and press Enter.
16 Key in your label document code and press Enter.

QUICK RESPONSE LABELS
Operations
OPERATIONS 2 GUIDE 4.2* 419

Quick Response Setup (QURE) screen showing Quick Response parameters for customer A, consignee CONS2
17 When you finish setting up your Quick Response parameters for this customer/consignee combination, 
click on Return to Main and Exit to exit.
Operations
Quick Response does not support multiple SKU classes on the same order. If you receive an order containing 
quantities in two SKU classes — for example, pallets and cases — you must convert one of your quantities. 
For example, if you specify cases as your SKU class in QURE, you must convert your pallet quantities to case 
quantities when entering your order.
PRINTING YOUR LABELS IN QRLE
You print your labels in the program QRLE (Quick Response Label Entry). You can print your Quick 
Response labels at any flow before COOR (Confirm Order). Printing quick response labels requires a bar 
code printer; you cannot print to a regular printer and you cannot print to VIEW.
In QRLE you specify three things: the number of labels that you wish to print, which items appear on which 
labels and the item quantities for each label. You can assign all your items to a single label, you can assign a 

QUICK RESPONSE LABELS
Operations
single item to each label, you can “mix and match” (that is, assign two items to one label, a third item to its 
own label, etc.) or you can assign a label to each case. 
You can also split an item between two or more labels. For example, you can assign 20 units of item A to one 
label and the remaining 10 units of the same item to a second label.
When you enter your number of labels, there are two options to choose from. If you enter 1, QRLE will 
automatically display all items on the order in the Label Detail Block. If you enter a number greater than 1, you 
will have to enter your items in the Label Detail Block of each label manually.
Label assigned to each item (no mixed product on 
pallet)
Label assigned to each case
NOTE If you are unsure about number of labels to print, enter a number that is 
lower than what you expect; for example, if you think that you will need five labels, 
enter four or three as your number of labels. If you need an additional label, you 
would enter five or six in the Number of Labels field to get one or two additional 
labels.
If you create too many labels and attempt to delete the extra labels, you will have to 
re-enter your item information manually for each label.
FIELD DESCRIPTIONS
Order Number Your order number.
Customer Order Number Your customer order number (if entered on order).
Number of Labels If you enter 1, QRLE will automatically display all items on the order in the 
Label Detail Block. If you enter a number greater than 1, you will have to enter 
your items in the Label Detail Block of each label manually.

QUICK RESPONSE LABELS
Operations
OPERATIONS 2 GUIDE 4.2* 421
Proof Fields
These fields show the number of units that have been assigned to labels. You cannot print your labels until 
all units have been assigned.
Total The total number of units on the order in the SKU class shown in the SKU 
Class field.
Entered The number of units that have been assigned to items.
Remaining The number of units that have NOT been assigned to any item.
SKU Class The SKU class that you specified in QURE (Quick Response Setup). All units 
in the Total, Entered and Remaining fields will be expressed in this SKU class.
Label Block
This block shows the Quick Response label number for each label. The only field that you can change in 
this block is the Type field.
Prefix The serial prefix that you defined in QURE (Quick Response Setup).
Type The type of shipping container for the label.
0 = case or carton
1 = pallet (larger than a case or carton)
2 = larger than a pallet
Member The member number that you defined in QURE (Quick Response Setup).
Serial Number A sequential number generated by AccellosOne 3PL for each label using your 
start and end numbers defined in QURE.
Check Digit Calculated according to MH10/UCC 128 specifications.
Label Detail Block
In this block, you assign your items and the number of units for those items to the label that you selected 
in the Label Block.
Item The item for which the label is being printed.
Level 2/3/4 If required, the level 2/3/4 values for the item.
FIELD DESCRIPTIONS

REPRINTING LABELS IN QRLR
You can reprint Quick Response labels in the program QRLR (Quick Response Label Reprint).
1 Enter QRLR.
Values in 
proof fields 
change as 
you add items

QUICK RESPONSE LABELS
Operations
 Key in your order number and press Enter.

Quick Response Label Reprint (QRLR) screen showing five labels
2 Proceed to select the labels that you wish to reprint. You can select all labels by clicking on Select All or 
you can select individual labels by using your arrow keys to position the cursor over the label to reprint 
and then clicking on Select/Deselect. If you wish to deselect a selected label, use your arrow keys and 
the Select/Deselect button.

DELETING LABELS
Deleting extra labels in QRLE is not recommended because you cannot delete individual labels. Instead, you 
must enter a lower value than the current value in the Number of Labels field. When you enter a lower value 
in QRLE (for example, you replace 10 with 8), the number of labels will be adjusted accordingly.
CAUTION When you delete a label, the system will erase your item information 
from each label in the Label Block and you will have to re-enter your item information 
manually for each label. As well, the system will generate new serial numbers for all 
labels on the order starting from the last serial number generated; as a result, there 
will be a gap between that last serial number used on the previous order and the first 
serial number used on the current order.

QUICK RESPONSE LABELS
Operations
OPERATIONS 2 GUIDE 4.2* 427
To avoid the need to delete extra labels, it is essential to enter a number that is lower than the expected 
number whenever you are unsure about the exact number of labels that you will need. For example, if you 
think that you will need 10 labels, initially enter 8 as your number of labels. If you need an additional label, 
change the 8 to 9. If 9 labels are not enough, change the 9 to 10. By working up one or two labels at a time, 
you will avoid the need to use the delete function.
USING THE CONTAINER WITHIN A CONTAINER FUNCTION
The Container within a Container function allows you to create Quick Response labels for non-inventory 
entities such as pallets, cages, shipping containers, etc. For example, you create 10 labels for 10 boxes and 
then put the 10 boxes on a single pallet. The Allow Container within Container function allows you to create 
an additional label for the pallet that contains information about the product on the pallet.
Labels assigned to both cases and container 
There is no limit on the number of container labels that you can create for a given item or a given order. You 
can create a container label for a pallet and then create a second container label for the shipping container in 
which the pallet is placed. Container labels may be blank — that is, have no product assigned to them — or 
may contain regular product. 
In the Label Detail Block of a container label, you enter the serial number of the previous label as your item 
code. The number of units for a container label is always zero because container labels do not represent 
inventory.

QUICK RESPONSE LABELS
Operations

QRLE screen showing serial number of previous label as item code
In order to use the Container within Container function, the Container within Container field in QURE must be 
set to Yes.
1 Enter QRLE and retrieve the order that requires the non-inventory labels.
2 If required, change the value in the Number of Labels field by keying in a new value and pressing Enter. 
3 Click on Label Block.
4 Use your arrow keys to access the correct label. 
5 If you wish to change the type of shipping container, press Enter to position your cursor in the Type field. 
Then key in 0 for Case or Carton, 1 for Pallet or 2 for Larger than Pallet and press Enter.
6 Click on Label Detail Block.
7 Click on Create Record and enter your first item in the normal manner.
8 When you finish entering your item, click on Return to Main and Label Block.
9 Select your next label and click on Label Detail Block.
10 Click on Create Record.
11 Press F10 to display the pick list, then click on Execute Query.

LOOKING UP THE STATUS OF A QUICK RESPONSE ORDER IN LOOR
There are four status messages for a Quick Response order in LOOR:
A = Always (Quick Response is mandatory for this order)
O = Optional (Quick Response is optional for this order)
S = Started (the Quick Response labels have been started)
C = Complete (the Quick Response labels have been printed)
If you have entered your label information in QRLE and the status of the Quick Response order is either S for 
Started or C for Complete, you can look up the Quick Response labels for the order in the Quick Response 
Block. This block shows the item code, level 2, 3 and 4 values (if any) and the quantity for each label.
1 Enter LOOR.
2 Retrieve the order whose Quick Response status you want to look up.
Look Up Orders (LOOR) screen showing order with a Quick Response status
3 If you have entered your label information in QRLE and the status of the Quick Response order is either 
S for Started or C for Complete, you can look up the Quick Response labels for the order. Press Enter to 
position your cursor in the Quick Response field. Then click on Quick Response to display the Quick 
Response Block.
